It is also a major challenge in social and cultural terms. Too many immigrants and refugees without work crawl together into the ghettos and create parallel societies. They do not come into contact with the outside world, and their children do not grow up in an everyday life where Mom and Dad get up in the morning and go to work, and that is in every way a bad situation. Basically, the Left believes, of course, that when refugees come to Denmark, the starting point should be that they should return and help rebuild their homeland when there is peace, but while they are here, they should contribute to the community by being in the labour market, and therefore we have also said before that this loosely formulated idea by the government in 37 hours is something that we can see ourselves in. The Minister mentioned earlier that he has visited my municipality, where I am chairman of the Employment Committee and have moved a little on this agenda, and when we can support the one that has been formulated, we can of course also support this resolution by the Conservatives. But this isn't gonna solve everything, and it's very important. It is a small step in the right direction, if anything, but in the Left we have greater ambitions, which is why I can also reveal here a late evening hour from Parliament's platform that we ourselves will soon come up with a number of proposals for more fundamental reforms in this area which go further than both what the government has proposed and also what Conservatives have proposed in this motion for a resolution. ParlaMint-DK_20201119201226	Thank you for the floor. The proposal for a law we are considering here, namely L 58, has the overall aim of amending the provisions of the organic law so that they are adapted to the future organic regulation. In addition, the proposal contains amendments which it is considered appropriate to implement when the law is nevertheless amended. In concrete terms, it is proposed to amend the provision on the Ecological Food Council, to tighten up the level of fines for certain breaches of ecological rules and to adapt the law's provision on fees linguistically. Finally, the draft law proposes a new provision on the interconnection of information from other authorities in contact with the ecology industry. The homepage is expected to be used to obtain information from other authorities in order to improve the risk assessment so that it can support the planning and implementation of physical and administrative checks. We all think this is very sensible, we have to say. Nor does the draft law change the provisions of the Organic Law on organic mass caterers and the eco-control mark, which we also know as the Ø label. It is of course important that there is always confidence in the ecology and in the Ø label. As we know that the fines have not been changed in the last 16 years, we also find it appropriate to have a more up-to-date level of fines. In addition, of course, we must also take seriously the fact that the National Audit Office has been involved. It has been asked to assess whether the sanctions were effective, reasonable and dissuasive enough. Now that I have the floor, I also think it is worth highlighting that the European Commission presented their land for the table strategy and the biodiversity strategy in the spring. This shows that the Commission's objective in the area of ecology is at least 25%. of EU agricultural land in 2030. That is a very good objective, and I think we can take pleasure in that in the Chamber. Fortunately, Denmark is very good when we talk organically; we are well advanced. ParlaMint-DK_20201119203401	Thank you, sir. Per Larsen. In this way, we have actually reached the new Minister for Agriculture and Fisheries, whom I would also like to congratulate here in the Chair on the new tasks, and good luck with that. The minister, please.
ParlaMint-DK_20201119203421	First of all, many thanks to the President and thanks to the rapporteurs for their warm welcome. I am very pleased, but also humble, with the task I face as a new Minister for Food, Agriculture and Fisheries, but I am very pleased with the kind reception that is being given in this Chamber today. I am sure we will have a very good and constructive cooperation. It is this year that we celebrate the 30th anniversary of our red island brand, the eco-label. It's a big success story. The Danish eco-label is as well-known among consumers in Denmark as one of the world's strongest brands Coca-Cola. That is truly impressive. First of all, we link the E-mark with credibility and trust in the organic product. This confidence is also reflected in the fact that consumers in Denmark are world champions in the purchase of organic products. During the covid-19 pandemic, consumers' demand for ecology has increased. Ecological National Association estimates, for example, that sales of organic food to private individuals increased by as much as 20%. during the decommissioning in spring. I think it is extremely positive that the Danes' confidence in the Ø label is at the top. It is absolutely essential that we safeguard this trust. The ecology industry today consists of more than 4 000 organic farmers, who ensure that, for example, there are can be covered up to a clean eco-morning table with everything, from bread, eggs and dairy products to fruit and vegetables. In addition, there are also the many jobs in retail shops as well as the over 2,400 food companies that are being ecologically controlled today. In recent years, many farmers have converted to ecology and the ecological area has grown considerably. Exports of Danish organic products have also been in great growth and continue to enjoy great potential, especially in Europe, where demand is increasing. As a new minister in this area, I very much appreciate this, as it also runs away with the Government's desire to double the ecological area, exports and Danes' consumption. It is important that we safeguard confidence in Danish ecology. When there are many new organic farmers and food producers, it is very important to ensure that the rules for organic production and sales of products are complied with. Violations can undermine the credibility of organic farming and organic food and ultimately destroy the trust that ecology enjoys among all of us. We therefore need to continue to protect the brand that Danish ecology stands for. Our state control of organic production in Denmark with the Ø label as a symbol ensures that we in Denmark comply with the control requirements of the EU's common ecological rules. It is a great advantage that the rules are the same for all Member States, both for competition and for consumer confidence. After many years of negotiations, there will now be a new ecological regulation. This bill aims precisely to adapt the existing Danish organic law to the new organic regulation. The new organic law therefore repeals certain provisions which are now directly regulated by the regulation. This concerns, among other things: laying down rules on authorisation. The Ecology Regulation should actually apply from 1 January 2021, but on the basis of a proposal from the European Commission, the European Parliament and the Council have just decided to postpone the date of application by 1 year, i.e. to 1 January 2022. The reason for this is that many of the so-called legislative acts setting out the degree of detail of the rules have been delayed, particularly because of the covid-19 situation. However, in order to be absolutely sure that Denmark had the legal basis in place as soon as the new ecology regulation was applied, we are considering today the proposed amendment to the organic law. In addition to the adaptation of the new regulations, the draft law contains changes which are deemed appropriate to implement when the law is amended anyway. The draft law provides for the respective control authorities in the field of ecology, i.e.: The Danish Agriculture Agency and the Danish Food and Agriculture Authority will have the opportunity to obtain and collate information from other authorities in contact with the organic industry. The information will be used for planning and implementing more risk-based controls than at present. In concrete terms, there may be medical information, lease agreements and infringements of animal welfare rules. It must ensure the best possible compliance with ecological rules. Following this, the new organic law will considerably increase the level of fines for breaches of certain provisions of the ecological rules. It must ensure compliance with ecological rules by making sanctions more effective, more proportionate and dissuasive. As the amount of the fines has not been regulated in the last 16 years, I think it is quite appropriate to have a more up-to-date level of fines. The Ecology Regulation is a voluntary scheme, but when you are in it you must also always be fully aware of the rules and the consequences in the event of an infringement. That is why consumers expect it to be important for the credibility of ecology. In addition, the new law provides the opportunity to revitalize the work carried out under the auspices of the Ecological Food Council. Experience with the Council has shown that greater flexibility is needed. The regulation of the ecology area is now so complex that in all situations the Organic Food Council is not the most appropriate forum to advise the Minister for Food at any time in relation to the further development of the ecology area. Instead, the Minister will have the opportunity to set up advisory committees to monitor developments in the ecology sector and to advise the Minister. Representatives of the entire value chain will be offered participation. Finally, the bill adapts the fee provisions of the law linguistically. The adjustment will not affect the fact that, as in the past, rules can be laid down for the collection of payments to cover, in whole or in part, the costs of, inter alia: control and administration. The government hopes that the Danish Parliament will support our proposal to amend the Organic Law. In these words, I look forward to a favourable committee reading and thank you for the debate and the good input that has been given here.
